\displaystyle I+\hbar S_{N}(\varepsilon,\hbar).

The formula for composition of operators and the Calderon-Vaillancourt theorem gives us that the operator
S_{N}
satisfies the estimate

\sup_{\hbar\in(0,\hbar_{0}]}\lVert S_{N}(\varepsilon,\hbar)\rVert_{\mathcal{L}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}<\infty

(4.1)

We note that if

\hbar
is chosen such that

\hbar\lVert S_{N}(\varepsilon,\hbar)\rVert_{\mathcal{L}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}<1
then
the operator
I+\hbar S_{N}(\varepsilon,\hbar)
will be
invertible.

The Calderon-Vaillancourt theorem gives us that

\OpW(b_{\varepsilon,t})
is a bounded operator. This implies that
the expression

\OpW(b_{\varepsilon,t})(I+\hbar S_{N}(\varepsilon,\hbar))^{-1}

is a well defined bounded operator. Hence we have that the operator

(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)-t)
maps its domain surjective onto all of

L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. By
[
21
, Proposition 3.11]
this implies that

A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)
is essential self-adjoint.

Since we have for all
t<\zeta_{0}
that

(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)-t)
maps its domain surjective onto all of

L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
they are all in the resolvent set and hence the operator
has to be lower semi-bounded.
∎

4.2
The resolvent of a rough pseudo-differential operator

A main part in the construction of the functional calculus is to prove that the resolvent of a
\hbar
-
\varepsilon
-admissible
operator of regularity
\tau
, which satisfies Assumption
4.1
, is an operator of the same type. this is the content of the following Theorem.

Theorem 4.3
.

Let
A(\hbar)
, for
\hbar
in
(0,\hbar_{0}]
, be a

\hbar
-
\varepsilon
-admissible operator of regularity

\tau\geq 1
with tempered weight
m
and symbol

a_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)=\sum_{j\geq 0}\hbar^{j}a_{\varepsilon,j}.

Suppose that
A_{\varepsilon}(h)
satisfies
Assumption
4.1
with the numbers
\zeta_{0}

and
\zeta_{1}
. For
z
in
\mathbb{C}\setminus[\zeta_{1},\infty)
we define
the sequence of symbols

\displaystyle b_{\varepsilon,z,0}

\displaystyle=(a_{\varepsilon,0}-z)^{-1}

(4.2)

\displaystyle b_{\varepsilon,z,j+1}

\displaystyle=-b_{\varepsilon,z,0}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l+\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|+k=j+1\\
0\leq l\leq j\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\alpha!\beta!}\frac{1}{2^{\left|\alpha\right|}}\frac{1}{(-2)^{\left|\beta\right|}}(\partial_{p}^{\alpha}D_{x}^{\beta}a_{\varepsilon,k})(\partial_{p}^{\beta}D_{x}^{\alpha}b_{\varepsilon,z,l}),

for
j\geq 1
. Moreover we define

B_{\varepsilon,z,M}(\hbar)=\sum_{j=0}^{M}\hbar^{j}b_{\varepsilon,z,j}.

Then for
N
in
\mathbb{N}
we have that

(A_{\varepsilon}(h)-z)\OpW B_{\varepsilon,z,N}=I+h^{N+1}\Delta_{z,N+1}(h),

(4.3)

with

\hbar^{N+1}\lVert\Delta_{z,N+1}(h)\rVert_{\mathcal{L}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\leq C\hbar^{\kappa(N)}\left(\frac{\left|z\right|}{\dist(z,[\zeta_{1},\infty))}\right)^{q(N)},

(4.4)

where
\kappa
is a positive strictly increasing function and
q(N)

is a positive integer depending on
N
. In particular we have for
all
z
in
\mathbb{C}\setminus[\zeta_{1},\infty)
and all
\hbar
in

(0,\hbar_{1}]
(
\hbar_{1}
sufficient small and independent of
z
),
that
(A_{\varepsilon}(h)-z)^{-1}
is a

\hbar
-
\varepsilon
-admissible operator with respect to the
tempered weight
(a_{\varepsilon,0}-\zeta_{1})^{-1}
and of
regularity
\tau
with symbol:

B_{z}(h)=\sum_{j\geq 0}h^{j}b_{\varepsilon,z,j}.

(4.5)

Before we can prove the theorem we will need some lemma’s with the
same setting. It is in these lemma’s we will see that the symbol for the resolvent has the same regularity. From these lemmas we can also find the explicit formula’s for every symbol in the expansion.

Lemma 4.4
.

Let the setting be as in Theorem
4.3
. For
every
j
in
\mathbb{N}
we have

b_{\varepsilon,z,j}=\sum_{k=1}^{2j-1}d_{\varepsilon,j,k}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1},

(4.6)

where
d_{\varepsilon,j,k}
are universal polynomials in

\partial_{p}^{\alpha}\partial_{x}^{\beta}a_{\varepsilon,l}
for

\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|+l\leq j
and

d_{\varepsilon,j,k}\in\Gamma_{0,\varepsilon}^{(a_{0}-\zeta_{1})^{k},\tau-j}
for all
k
,

1\leq k\leq 2j-1
. In particular we have that

b_{\varepsilon,z,1}=-a_{\varepsilon,1}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{2}.

In order to prove this Lemma we will be needing the following Lemma:

Lemma 4.5
.

Let the setting be as in
Lemma
4.4
. For any
j
and
k
in

\mathbb{N}
we let

d_{\varepsilon,j,k}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1}
be one of the
elements in the expansion of
b_{\varepsilon,z,j}
. Then for all
multi indices
\alpha
and
\beta
it holds that

\partial_{p}^{\beta}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}d_{\varepsilon,j,k}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1}=\sum_{n=0}^{\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|}\tilde{d}_{\varepsilon,j,k,n,\alpha,\beta}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1+n},

where
\tilde{d}_{\varepsilon,j,k,n,\alpha,\beta}
are
polynomials in

\partial_{x}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\partial_{p}^{\beta^{\prime}}a_{\varepsilon,k}
with

\left|\alpha^{\prime}\right|+\left|\beta^{\prime}\right|+k\leq j+\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|
of
degree at most
k+n
and they are of regularity at least

\tau-j-\left|\alpha\right|
. They are determined only by
\alpha
,
\beta

and
d_{\varepsilon,j,k}
.

Proof.

The proof is an application of Theorem
A.1
(Faà
di Bruno formula) and the Corollary
A.2
to the
formula. For our
\alpha,\beta
we have by the Leibniz’s formula that:

\displaystyle\partial_{p}^{\beta}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}d_{\varepsilon,j,k}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1}=\partial_{p}^{\beta}\big\{(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}d_{\varepsilon,j,k})b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\gamma\right|=1\\
\gamma\leq\alpha\end{subarray}}^{\left|\alpha\right|}\binom{\alpha}{\gamma}\partial_{x}^{\alpha-\gamma}d_{\varepsilon,j,k}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1}\big\}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle(\partial_{p}^{\beta}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}d_{\varepsilon,j,k})b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\zeta\right|=1\\
\zeta\leq\beta\end{subarray}}^{\left|\beta\right|}\binom{\beta}{\zeta}\partial_{p}^{\beta-\zeta}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}d_{\varepsilon,j,k}\partial_{p}^{\zeta}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\gamma\right|=1\\
\gamma\leq\alpha\end{subarray}}^{\left|\alpha\right|}\binom{\alpha}{\gamma}(\partial_{p}^{\beta}\partial_{x}^{\alpha-\gamma}d_{\varepsilon,j,k})\partial_{x}^{\gamma}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\zeta\right|=1\\
\zeta\leq\beta\end{subarray}}^{\left|\beta\right|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\gamma\right|=1\\
\gamma\leq\alpha\end{subarray}}^{\left|\alpha\right|}\binom{\beta}{\zeta}\binom{\alpha}{\gamma}(\partial_{p}^{\beta-\zeta}\partial_{x}^{\alpha-\gamma}d_{\varepsilon,j,k})\partial_{p}^{\zeta}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1}.

We will here consider each of the three sums separately for the
first we get by the Faà di Bruno formula
(Theorem
A.1
)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\zeta\right|=1\\
\zeta\leq\beta\end{subarray}}^{\left|\beta\right|}\binom{\beta}{\zeta}\partial_{p}^{\beta-\zeta}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}d_{\varepsilon,j,k}\partial_{p}^{\zeta}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\zeta\right|=1\\
\zeta\leq\beta\end{subarray}}^{\left|\beta\right|}\binom{\beta}{\zeta}\partial_{p}^{\beta-\zeta}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}d_{\varepsilon,j,k}\sum_{{n}=1}^{\left|\zeta\right|}(-1)^{n}\frac{(k+n)!}{k!}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1+n}\;\;\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\zeta_{1}+\cdots+\zeta_{n}=\zeta\\
\left|\zeta_{i}\right|>0\end{subarray}}}\;\;c_{\zeta_{1},\dots,\zeta_{n}}\partial_{p}^{\zeta_{1}}a_{0}\cdots\partial_{p}^{\zeta_{n}}a_{0}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n_{\beta}=1}^{\left|\beta\right|}\Big\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\zeta\right|\geq n_{\beta}\\
\zeta\leq\beta\end{subarray}}^{\left|\beta\right|}c_{k,n_{\beta},\beta,\zeta}\partial_{p}^{\beta-\zeta}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}d_{\varepsilon,j,k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\zeta_{1}+\cdots+\zeta_{n_{\beta}}=\zeta\\
\left|\zeta_{i}\right|>0\end{subarray}}c_{\zeta_{1},\dots,\zeta_{n}}\partial_{p}^{\zeta_{1}}a_{0}\cdots\partial_{p}^{\zeta_{n_{\beta}}}a_{0}\Big\}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1+n_{\beta}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n_{\beta}=1}^{\left|\beta\right|}\tilde{d}_{\varepsilon,j,k,\alpha,\beta,n_{\beta}}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1+n_{\beta}}.

This calculation gives that we have a polynomial structure, where the coefficients are
the polynomials
\tilde{d}_{\varepsilon,j,k,\alpha,\beta,n_{\beta}}
, which themselfs
are polynomials in

\partial_{x}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\partial_{p}^{\beta^{\prime}}a_{\varepsilon,k}
with

\left|\alpha^{\prime}\right|+\left|\beta^{\prime}\right|+k\leq j+\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|
and
of regularity
\tau-j-\left|\alpha\right|
.
For the second sum we again use Faà di Bruno formula
(Theorem
A.1
) and get

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\gamma\right|=1\\
\gamma\leq\alpha\end{subarray}}^{\left|\alpha\right|}\binom{\alpha}{\gamma}(\partial_{p}^{\beta}\partial_{x}^{\alpha-\gamma}d_{\varepsilon,j,k})\partial_{x}^{\gamma}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\gamma\right|=1\\
\gamma\leq\alpha\end{subarray}}^{\left|\alpha\right|}\binom{\alpha}{\gamma}\partial_{p}^{\beta}\partial_{x}^{\alpha-\gamma}d_{\varepsilon,j,k}\sum_{{n}=1}^{\left|\gamma\right|}(-1)^{n}\frac{(k+n)!}{k!}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1+n}\quad\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma_{1}+\cdots+\gamma_{n}=\gamma\\
\left|\gamma_{i}\right|>0\end{subarray}}}\;c_{\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{n}}\partial_{x}^{\gamma_{1}}a_{0}\cdots\partial_{x}^{\gamma_{n}}a_{0}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{n_{\alpha}=1}^{\left|\alpha\right|}\Big\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\gamma\right|\geq n_{\alpha}\\
\gamma\leq\alpha\end{subarray}}^{\left|\alpha\right|}c_{k,n_{\alpha},\alpha,\gamma}\partial_{p}^{\beta}\partial_{x}^{\alpha-\gamma}d_{\varepsilon,j,k}\quad\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma_{1}+\cdots+\gamma_{n_{\alpha}}=\gamma\\
\left|\gamma_{i}\right|>0\end{subarray}}}\;c_{\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{n}}\partial_{x}^{\gamma_{1}}a_{0}\cdots\partial_{x}^{\gamma_{n_{\alpha}}}a_{0}\Big\}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1+n_{\alpha}}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{n_{\alpha}=1}^{\left|\alpha\right|}\tilde{d}_{\varepsilon,j,k,\alpha,\beta,n_{\alpha}}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1+n_{\alpha}}.

Again we have the polynomial structure,where the coefficients are
the polynomials
\tilde{d}_{\varepsilon,j,k,\alpha,\beta,n_{\alpha}}
again
are polynomials in

\partial_{x}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\partial_{p}^{\beta^{\prime}}a_{\varepsilon,k}
with

\left|\alpha^{\prime}\right|+\left|\beta^{\prime}\right|+k\leq j+\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|
and
they are of at least regularity
\tau-j-\left|\alpha\right|
.

For the last sum we need a slightly modified version of the Faà di
Bruno formula which is Corollary
A.2
. If we
use this we get that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\zeta\right|=1\\
\zeta\leq\beta\end{subarray}}^{\left|\beta\right|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\gamma\right|=1\\
\gamma\leq\alpha\end{subarray}}^{\left|\alpha\right|}\binom{\beta}{\zeta}\binom{\alpha}{\gamma}(\partial_{p}^{\beta-\zeta}\partial_{x}^{\alpha-\gamma}d_{\varepsilon,j,k})\partial_{p}^{\zeta}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\zeta\right|=1\\
\zeta\leq\beta\end{subarray}}^{\left|\beta\right|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\gamma\right|=1\\
\gamma\leq\alpha\end{subarray}}^{\left|\alpha\right|}\binom{\beta}{\zeta}\binom{\alpha}{\gamma}(\partial_{p}^{\beta-\zeta}\partial_{x}^{\alpha-\gamma}d_{\varepsilon,j,k})\sum_{n=1}^{\left|\zeta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|}c_{n}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1+n}\sum_{\mathcal{I}_{n}(\gamma,\zeta)}c_{\gamma_{1}\cdots\gamma_{k}}^{\zeta_{1}\cdots\zeta_{k}}\partial_{p}^{\zeta_{1}}\partial_{x}^{\gamma_{1}}a_{\varepsilon,0}\cdots\partial_{p}^{\zeta_{n}}\partial_{x}^{\gamma_{n}}a_{\varepsilon,0}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|}\Big\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\zeta\right|+\left|\gamma\right|\geq n\\
\zeta\leq\beta,\ \gamma\leq\alpha\end{subarray}}^{\left|\beta\right|+\left|\alpha\right|}\sum_{\mathcal{I}_{n}(\gamma,\zeta)}c_{k,n,\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\zeta}(\partial_{p}^{\beta-\zeta}\partial_{x}^{\alpha-\gamma}d_{\varepsilon,j,k})\partial_{p}^{\zeta_{1}}\partial_{x}^{\gamma_{1}}a_{\varepsilon,0}\cdots\partial_{p}^{\zeta_{n}}\partial_{x}^{\gamma_{n}}a_{\varepsilon,0}\Big\}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1+n}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|}\tilde{d}_{\varepsilon,j,k,n,\alpha,\beta}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1+n},

where
\tilde{d}_{\varepsilon,j,k,n,\alpha,\beta}
are polynomials
in
\partial_{x}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\partial_{p}^{\beta^{\prime}}a_{\varepsilon,k}
with

\left|\alpha^{\prime}\right|+\left|\beta^{\prime}\right|+k\leq j+\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|
of
degree at most
k+n
and they are of regularity at least

\tau-j-\left|\alpha\right|
. If we combine all of the above calculations
we get the desired result:

\partial_{p}^{\beta}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}d_{\varepsilon,j,k}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1}=\sum_{n=0}^{\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|}\tilde{d}_{\varepsilon,j,k,n,\alpha,\beta}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1+n},

where
\tilde{d}_{\varepsilon,j,k,n,\alpha,\beta}
are
polynomials in

\partial_{x}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\partial_{p}^{\beta^{\prime}}a_{\varepsilon,k}
with

\left|\alpha^{\prime}\right|+\left|\beta^{\prime}\right|+k\leq j+\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|
of
degree at most
k+n
and they are of regularity at least

\tau-j-\left|\alpha\right|
. The form of the polynomials is entirely
determined by the multi-indices
\alpha,\beta
and the polynomial

d_{\varepsilon,j,k}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.4
.

The proof will be induction in the parameter
j
. We start by
considering the case
j=1
, where we by definition of

b_{\varepsilon,z,1}
have

\displaystyle b_{\varepsilon,z,1}

\displaystyle=-b_{\varepsilon,z,0}\cdot\sum_{\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|+k=1}\frac{1}{\alpha!\beta!}\frac{1}{2^{\left|\alpha\right|}}\frac{1}{(-2)^{\left|\beta\right|}}(\partial_{p}^{\alpha}D_{x}^{\beta}a_{\varepsilon,k})(\partial_{p}^{\beta}D_{x}^{\alpha}b_{\varepsilon,z,0})

\displaystyle=-b_{\varepsilon,z,0}\big(a_{\varepsilon,1}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}-\frac{i}{2}\sum_{n=1}^{d}\partial_{p_{n}}a_{\varepsilon,0}\partial_{x_{n}}a_{\varepsilon,0}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{2}-\partial_{x_{n}}a_{\varepsilon,0}\partial_{p_{n}}a_{\varepsilon,0}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle=-a_{\varepsilon,1}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{2}.

This calculation verifies the form of
b_{\varepsilon,z,1}
stated
in the lemma and that it has the form given by
(
4.6
) with

d_{\varepsilon,1,1}=-a_{\varepsilon,1}
which is in the symbol
class
\Gamma_{0,\varepsilon}^{(a_{0}-\zeta_{1}),\tau-1}
by assumption.

Assume the lemma to be correct for
b_{\varepsilon,z,j}
and
consider
b_{\varepsilon,z,j+1}
. By the definition of

b_{\varepsilon,z,j+1}
and our assumption we have

\displaystyle b_{\varepsilon,z,j+1}={}

\displaystyle-b_{\varepsilon,z,0}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l+\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|+k=j+1\\
0\leq l\leq j\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\alpha!\beta!}\frac{1}{2^{\left|\alpha\right|}}\frac{1}{(-2)^{\left|\beta\right|}}(\partial_{p}^{\alpha}D_{x}^{\beta}a_{\varepsilon,k})(\partial_{p}^{\beta}D_{x}^{\alpha}b_{\varepsilon,z,l})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-b_{\varepsilon,z,0}\Big\{\sum_{\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|+k=j+1}c_{\alpha,\beta}(\partial_{p}^{\alpha}D_{x}^{\beta}a_{\varepsilon,k})(\partial_{p}^{\beta}D_{x}^{\alpha}b_{\varepsilon,z,0})

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=1}^{j}\sum_{m=1}^{2l-1}\sum_{l+\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|+k=j+1}c_{\alpha,\beta}(\partial_{p}^{\alpha}D_{x}^{\beta}a_{\varepsilon,k})(\partial_{p}^{\beta}D_{x}^{\alpha}d_{\varepsilon,l,m}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{m+1})\Big\}.

We will consider each of the sums separately. To calculate the first
sum we get by applying Corollary
A.2

\displaystyle\sum_{\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|+k=j+1}c_{\alpha,\beta}(\partial_{p}^{\alpha}D_{x}^{\beta}a_{\varepsilon,k})(\partial_{p}^{\beta}D_{x}^{\alpha}b_{\varepsilon,z,0})-c_{\alpha,\beta}a_{\varepsilon,j+1}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|+k=j+1\\
\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|\geq 1\end{subarray}}\Big\{c_{\alpha,\beta}(\partial_{p}^{\alpha}D_{x}^{\beta}a_{\varepsilon,k})\sum_{n=1}^{\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|}c_{n}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{n+1}\sum_{\mathcal{I}_{n}(\alpha,\beta)}c_{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{n}}^{\beta_{1}\cdots\beta_{n}}\partial_{p}^{\beta_{1}}D_{x}^{\alpha_{1}}a_{\varepsilon,0}\cdots\partial_{p}^{\beta_{n}}D_{x}^{\alpha_{n}}a_{\varepsilon,0}\Big\}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{j+1}\Big\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|+k=j+1\\
\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|\geq n\end{subarray}}\sum_{\mathcal{I}_{n}(\alpha,\beta)}c_{\alpha,\beta,n}(\partial_{p}^{\alpha}D_{x}^{\beta}a_{\varepsilon,k})\partial_{p}^{\beta_{1}}D_{x}^{\alpha_{1}}a_{\varepsilon,0}\cdots\partial_{p}^{\beta_{n}}D_{x}^{\alpha_{n}}a_{\varepsilon,0}\Big\}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{n+1}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{j+1}\tilde{d}_{\varepsilon,j,n,\alpha,\beta}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{n+1}-c_{\alpha,\beta}a_{\varepsilon,j+1}b_{\varepsilon,z,0},

where
\tilde{d}_{\varepsilon,j,n,\alpha,\beta}
are
polynomials in

\partial_{x}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\partial_{p}^{\beta^{\prime}}a_{\varepsilon,k}
with

\left|\alpha^{\prime}\right|+\left|\beta^{\prime}\right|+k\leq j+1
of degree
n+1
and of
regularity at least
\tau-j-1
. The index set
\mathcal{I}_{n}(\alpha,\beta)
is defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{n}(\alpha,\beta)=\{(\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{n},

\displaystyle\beta_{1},\dots,\beta_{n})\in\mathbb{N}^{2nd}\

\displaystyle|\ \sum_{l=1}^{n}\alpha_{l}=\alpha,\,\sum_{l=1}^{n}\beta_{l}=\beta,\,\max(\left|\alpha_{l}\right|,\left|\beta_{l}\right|)\geq 1\,\forall l\}.

The form of the polynomials is
determined by the multi indices
\alpha
and
\beta
. Moreover we
have that the polynomials

\tilde{d}_{\varepsilon,j,n,\alpha,\beta}
are elements of

\Gamma_{0,\varepsilon}^{(a_{0}-\zeta_{1})^{n},\tau-j-1}
.

If we now consider the triple sum and apply
Lemma
4.5
we get

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{j}\sum_{m=1}^{2l-1}\sum_{l+\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|+k=j+1}c_{\alpha,\beta}(\partial_{p}^{\alpha}D_{x}^{\beta}a_{\varepsilon,k})(\partial_{p}^{\beta}D_{x}^{\alpha}d_{\varepsilon,l,m}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{m+1})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{j}\sum_{m=1}^{2l-1}\sum_{l+\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|+k=j+1}c_{\alpha,\beta}(\partial_{p}^{\alpha}D_{x}^{\beta}a_{\varepsilon,k})(-i)^{\left|\alpha\right|}\sum_{n=0}^{\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|}\tilde{d}_{\varepsilon,l,m,n,\alpha,\beta}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{m+1+n}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{2j-1}\sum_{l=\lceil\frac{m-1}{2}\rceil+1}^{j}\sum_{l+\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|+k=j+1}\sum_{n=0}^{\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|}c_{\alpha,\beta}(\partial_{p}^{\alpha}D_{x}^{\beta}a_{\varepsilon,k})(-i)^{\left|\alpha\right|}\tilde{d}_{\varepsilon,l,m,n,\alpha,\beta}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{m+1+n}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{2j-1}\sum_{l=\lceil\frac{m-1}{2}\rceil+1}^{j}\sum_{n=0}^{j+1-l}\sum_{\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|=n}c_{\alpha,\beta}(\partial_{p}^{\alpha}D_{x}^{\beta}a_{\varepsilon,j+1-l-n})(-i)^{\left|\alpha\right|}\tilde{d}_{\varepsilon,l,m,n,\alpha,\beta}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{m+1+n},

where the
\tilde{d}_{\varepsilon,l,m,n\alpha,\beta}
are the
polynomials from Lemma
4.5
. The way
we have expressed the sums ensures
k
always is the fixed value

j+1-l-n
. From Lemma
4.5
we have
that
\tilde{d}_{\varepsilon,l,m,n,\alpha,\beta}
are
polynomials in

\partial_{x}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\partial_{p}^{\beta^{\prime}}a_{\varepsilon,m}
with

\left|\alpha^{\prime}\right|+\left|\beta^{\prime}\right|+m\leq l+n\leq j+1
of degree
n+m
and
with regularity at least
\tau-l-\left|\alpha\right|
. Hence the factors

c_{\alpha,\beta}(\partial_{p}^{\alpha}D_{x}^{\beta}a_{\varepsilon,j+1-l-n})(-i)^{\left|\alpha\right|}\tilde{d}_{\varepsilon,l,m,n,\alpha,\beta}
will be polynomials in

\partial_{x}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\partial_{p}^{\beta^{\prime}}a_{\varepsilon,m}
with

\left|\alpha^{\prime}\right|+\left|\beta^{\prime}\right|+m\leq j+1
of degree
n+m+1
. The
regularity of the terms will be at least

\tau-l-\left|\alpha\right|-(j+1-l-n)-\left|\beta\right|=\tau-(j+1),

where most terms will have more regularity. By rewriting and
renaming some of the terms we get the following equality

\sum_{l=1}^{j}\sum_{m=1}^{2l-1}\sum_{l+\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|+k=j+1}c_{\alpha,\beta}(\partial_{p}^{\alpha}D_{x}^{\beta}a_{\varepsilon,k})(\partial_{p}^{\beta}D_{x}^{\alpha}d_{\varepsilon,l,m}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{m+1})=\sum_{n=1}^{2j}\tilde{d}_{\varepsilon,j,n,\alpha,\beta}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{n+1},

where
\tilde{d}_{\varepsilon,j,n,\alpha,\beta}
again are
polynomials in

\partial_{x}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\partial_{p}^{\beta^{\prime}}a_{\varepsilon,k}
with

\left|\alpha^{\prime}\right|+\left|\beta^{\prime}\right|+k\leq j+1
of degree
n+1
of
regularity at least
\tau-(j+1)
. By combing these calculation we
arrive at the expression

\displaystyle b_{\varepsilon,z,j+1}={}

\displaystyle-b_{\varepsilon,z,0}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l+\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|+k=j+1\\
0\leq l\leq j\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\alpha!\beta!}\frac{1}{2^{\left|\alpha\right|}}\frac{1}{(-2)^{\left|\beta\right|}}(\partial_{p}^{\alpha}D_{x}^{\beta}a_{\varepsilon,k})(\partial_{p}^{\beta}D_{x}^{\alpha}b_{\varepsilon,z,l})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-b_{\varepsilon,z,0}\Big\{\sum_{n=0}^{j+1}\tilde{d}_{\varepsilon,j,n,\alpha,\beta}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{n+1}+\sum_{n=1}^{2j}\tilde{d}_{\varepsilon,j,n,\alpha,\beta}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{n+1}\Big\}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{2j+1}d_{\varepsilon,j+1,k}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1},

where the polynomials
d_{\varepsilon,j+1,k}
are universal
polynomials in

\partial_{x}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\partial_{p}^{\beta^{\prime}}a_{\varepsilon,k}
with

\left|\alpha^{\prime}\right|+\left|\beta^{\prime}\right|+k\leq j+1
of degree
k
and with
regularity at least
\tau-j-1
. Hence they are elements of

\Gamma_{0,\varepsilon}^{(a_{0}-\zeta_{1})^{k},\tau-(j+1)}
. This ends
the proof.
∎

Lemma 4.6
.

Let the setting be as in Theorem
4.3
. For
every
j
in
\mathbb{N}
and
\alpha
,
\beta
in
\mathbb{N}^{d}
there exists a
number
c_{j,\alpha,\beta}>0
such that

\left|\partial_{p}^{\beta}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}b_{\varepsilon,z,j}\right|\leq c_{j,\alpha,\beta}\varepsilon^{-(\tau-j-\left|\alpha\right|)_{-}}(a_{\varepsilon,0}-\zeta_{1})^{-1}\left(\frac{\left|z-\zeta_{1}\right|}{\dist(z,[\zeta_{1},\infty))}\right)^{2j+\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|},

for all
z\in\mathbb{C}\setminus[\zeta_{1},\infty)
and all

(x,p)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}_{p}
.

Proof.

We start by considering the fraction

\frac{\left|a_{\varepsilon,0}-\zeta_{1}\right|}{\left|a_{\varepsilon,0}-z\right|}

and we will divide it into two cases according to the real part of

z
. If
\re(z)<\zeta_{1}
then

\frac{\left|a_{\varepsilon,0}-\zeta_{1}\right|}{\left|a_{\varepsilon,0}-z\right|}\leq 1\leq\frac{\left|z-\zeta_{1}\right|}{\dist(z,[\zeta_{1},\infty))}.

If instead
\re(z)\geq\zeta_{1}
and
\left|\im{z}\right|>0
we have by the
law of sines that

\frac{\left|a_{\varepsilon,0}-z\right|}{\sin(\phi_{1})}=\frac{\left|a_{\varepsilon,0}-\zeta_{1}\right|}{\sin(\phi_{2})}\geq\left|a_{\varepsilon,0}-\zeta_{1}\right|,

where
\phi_{1}
and
\phi_{2}
are angles in the triangle with vertices
\zeta_{1}
,
a_{\varepsilon,0}
and
z
. We have in the above estimate used that
0<\sin(\phi_{2})\leq 1
. If we apply this
inequality and the law of sines again we arrive at the following
expression

\frac{\left|a_{\varepsilon,0}-\zeta_{1}\right|}{\left|a_{\varepsilon,0}-z\right|}\leq\frac{1}{\sin(\phi_{1})}=\frac{\left|z-\zeta_{1}\right|}{\left|\im(z)\right|}.

Combining these two cases we get the estimate

\frac{\left|a_{\varepsilon,0}-\zeta_{1}\right|}{\left|a_{\varepsilon,0}-z\right|}\leq\frac{\left|z-\zeta_{1}\right|}{\dist(z,[\zeta_{1},\infty))}\quad\quad\text{For all $z\in\mathbb{C}\setminus[\zeta_{1},\infty)$.}

(4.7)

If we now consider a
given
b_{\varepsilon,z,j}
and
\alpha,\beta
in

\mathbb{N}^{d}
. Lemma
4.4
and
Lemma
4.5
gives us

\partial_{p}^{\beta}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}b_{\varepsilon,z,j}=\sum_{k=1}^{2j-1}\partial_{p}^{\beta}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(d_{\varepsilon,j,k}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1})=\sum_{k=1}^{2j-1}\sum_{n=0}^{\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|}\tilde{d}_{\varepsilon,j,k,n,\alpha,\beta}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1+n},

with
\tilde{d}_{\varepsilon,j,k,\alpha,\beta}
in

\Gamma_{0,\varepsilon}^{(a_{0}-\zeta_{1})^{k+n},\tau-j-\left|\alpha\right|}
. By
taking absolute value and applying (
4.7
) we get
that

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{p}^{\beta}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}b_{\varepsilon,z,j}\right|\leq{}

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{2j-1}\sum_{n=0}^{\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|}\left|\tilde{d}_{\varepsilon,j,k,n,\alpha,\beta}b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1+n}\right|

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\left|b_{\varepsilon,z,0}\right|\sum_{k=1}^{2j-1}\sum_{n=0}^{\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|}\varepsilon^{-(\tau-j-\left|\alpha\right|)_{-}}c_{j,k,\alpha,\beta}\left(\frac{\left|a_{\varepsilon,0}-\zeta_{1}\right|}{\left|a_{\varepsilon,0}-z\right|}\right)^{k+n}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle c_{j,\alpha,\beta}\varepsilon^{-(\tau-j-\left|\alpha\right|)_{-}}(a_{\varepsilon,0}-\zeta_{1})^{-1}\left(\frac{\left|z-\zeta_{1}\right|}{\dist(z,[\zeta_{1},\infty))}\right)^{2j+\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|},

where we have use that

\left|b_{\varepsilon,z,0}\right|=\frac{(a_{\varepsilon,0}-\zeta_{1})}{|a_{\varepsilon,0}-z|(a_{\varepsilon,0}-\zeta_{1})}\leq\frac{1}{(a_{\varepsilon,0}-\zeta_{1})}\left(\frac{\left|z-\zeta_{1}\right|}{\dist(z,[\zeta_{1},\infty))}\right).

We have now obtained the desired estimate and this ends the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.3
.

By Lemma
4.4
the
symbols
b_{\varepsilon,z,j}
are in the class

\Gamma_{0,\varepsilon}^{(a_{0}-\zeta_{1})^{-1},\tau-j}
for every
j

in
\mathbb{N}
, where
b_{\varepsilon,z,j}
is defined
(
4.2
). Hence we have that

B_{\varepsilon,z,N}(\hbar)=\sum_{j=0}^{N}\hbar^{j}b_{\varepsilon,z,j}.

is a well defined symbol for every
N
in
\mathbb{N}
. Moreover as

(a_{0}-\zeta_{1})^{-1}
is a bounded function we have by
Theorem
3.25
that
\OpW(B_{\varepsilon,z,N}(\hbar))

is a bounded operator. Now for
N
sufficiently large we have by
assumption

A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)-z=\OpW(a_{\varepsilon,0}-z)+\sum_{k=1}^{N}\hbar^{k}\OpW(a_{\varepsilon,k})+\hbar^{N+1}R_{N}(\varepsilon,\hbar),

where the error term satisfies

\hbar^{N+1}\lVert R_{N}(\varepsilon,\hbar)\rVert_{\mathcal{L}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\leq\hbar^{\kappa(N)}C_{N}

for a positive strictly increasing function
\kappa
. If we consider
the composition of
A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)
and

\OpW(B_{\varepsilon,z,N}(\hbar))
we get

\displaystyle A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)\OpW(B_{\varepsilon,z,N}(\hbar))

(4.8)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{N}\hbar^{k}\OpW(a_{\varepsilon,k})\sum_{j=0}^{N}\hbar^{j}\OpW(b_{\varepsilon,z,j})+\sum_{j=0}^{N}\hbar^{N+1+j}R_{N}(\varepsilon,\hbar)\OpW(b_{\varepsilon,z,j}).

If we consider the first part then this corresponds to a composition
of two strongly
\hbar
-
\varepsilon
-admissible operators. As we
want to apply Theorem
3.24
we need to ensure

N
satisfies the inequality

\delta(N+2d+2-\tau)+\tau>2d+1.

As this is the condition that ensures a positive power in front of
the error term. If we assume
N
satisfies the inequality. Then by
Theorem
3.24
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{N}\hbar^{k}\OpW(a_{\varepsilon,k})\sum_{j=0}^{N}\hbar^{j}\OpW(b_{\varepsilon,z,j})

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{N}\hbar^{l}\OpW(c_{\varepsilon,l})+\hbar^{N+1}\OpW(\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}(a_{\varepsilon}(\hbar),B_{\varepsilon},z,N(\hbar);\hbar)),

where

c_{\varepsilon,l}(x,p)=\sum_{\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|+k+j=l}\frac{1}{\alpha!\beta!}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big)^{\left|\alpha\right|}\Big(-\frac{1}{2}\Big)^{\left|\beta\right|}(\partial_{p}^{\alpha}D_{x}^{\beta}a_{\varepsilon,k})(\partial_{p}^{\beta}D_{x}^{\alpha}b_{\varepsilon,z,j})(x,p).

The error term satisfies for every multi indices
\alpha
,
\beta

in
\mathbb{N}^{d}
, that there exists a constant
C(\alpha,\beta,N)
independent of

a_{\varepsilon}
and
B_{\varepsilon,z,N}
and an integer
M
such
that

\displaystyle\hbar^{N+1}|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{p}^{\beta}\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}(a_{\varepsilon}(\hbar),b_{\varepsilon}(\hbar);x,p;\hbar)|

\displaystyle\leq C(\alpha,\beta,N)\hbar^{\delta(\tau-N-2d-2)_{-}+\tau-2d-1}\varepsilon^{-\left|\alpha\right|}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{j+k\leq 2N}\mathcal{G}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M,\tau-j-k}(a_{\varepsilon,k},(a_{\varepsilon,0}-\zeta_{1}),b_{\varepsilon,z,j},(a_{\varepsilon,0}-\zeta_{1})^{-1}),

where
\mathcal{G}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M,\tau-j-k}
are as defined in
Theorem
3.24
. By
Lemma
4.6
we have for all

j+k\leq 2N

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M,\tau-j-k}(a_{\varepsilon,k},(a_{\varepsilon,0}-\zeta_{1}),b_{\varepsilon,z,j},(a_{\varepsilon,0}-\zeta_{1})^{-1})

\displaystyle=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}\right|+\left|\eta_{1}+\eta_{2}\right|\leq M\\
(x,\xi)\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}\end{subarray}}\varepsilon^{(\tau-j-k-M)_{-}+\left|\alpha\right|}\left|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}(\partial_{x}^{\gamma_{1}}\partial_{\xi}^{\eta_{1}}a_{\varepsilon,k}(x,\xi)\partial_{x}^{\gamma_{2}}\partial_{\xi}^{\eta_{2}}b_{\varepsilon,z,j}(x,\xi))\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha,\beta,M}\;\sup_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}\right|+\left|\eta_{1}+\eta_{2}\right|\leq M\\
(x,\xi)\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}\end{subarray}}}\;\varepsilon^{(\tau-j-k-M)_{-}-(\tau-k-\gamma_{1})_{-}-(\tau-j-\gamma_{2})_{-}}\left(\frac{\left|z-\zeta_{1}\right|}{\dist(z,[\zeta_{1},\infty))}\right)^{2j+M+\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha,\beta,M}\left(\frac{\left|z-\zeta_{1}\right|}{\dist(z,[\zeta_{1},\infty))}\right)^{2j+M+\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|}.

Now by Theorem
3.25

there exists a number
M_{d}
such
that

\hbar^{N+1}\lVert\OpW(\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}(a_{\varepsilon}(\hbar),B_{\varepsilon},z,N(\hbar);\hbar))\rVert_{\mathcal{L}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\leq C\hbar^{\kappa(N)}\left(\frac{\left|z-\zeta_{1}\right|}{\dist(z,[\zeta_{1},\infty))}\right)^{M_{d}}.

If we now consider the symbols
c_{\varepsilon,l}(x,p)
for

0\leq l\leq N
. For
l=0
we have

c_{\varepsilon,0}(x,p)=(a_{\varepsilon,0}(x,p)-z)b_{\varepsilon,z,0}(x,p)=1,

by definition of
b_{\varepsilon,z,0}(x,p)
. Now for

1\leq l\leq N
we have

\displaystyle c_{\varepsilon,l}={}

\displaystyle\sum_{\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|+k+j=l}\frac{1}{\alpha!\beta!}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big)^{\left|\alpha\right|}\Big(-\frac{1}{2}\Big)^{\left|\beta\right|}(\partial_{p}^{\alpha}D_{x}^{\beta}a_{\varepsilon,k})(\partial_{p}^{\beta}D_{x}^{\alpha}b_{\varepsilon,z,j})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|+k+j=l\\
0\leq j\leq l-1\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\alpha!\beta!}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big)^{\left|\alpha\right|}\Big(-\frac{1}{2}\Big)^{\left|\beta\right|}(\partial_{p}^{\alpha}D_{x}^{\beta}a_{\varepsilon,k})(\partial_{p}^{\beta}D_{x}^{\alpha}b_{\varepsilon,z,j})+(a_{\varepsilon,0}-z)b_{\varepsilon,z,l}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|+k+j=l\\
0\leq j\leq l-1\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\alpha!\beta!}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big)^{\left|\alpha\right|}\Big(-\frac{1}{2}\Big)^{\left|\beta\right|}(\partial_{p}^{\alpha}D_{x}^{\beta}a_{\varepsilon,k})(\partial_{p}^{\beta}D_{x}^{\alpha}b_{\varepsilon,z,j})

\displaystyle-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left|\alpha\right|+\left|\beta\right|+k+j=l\\
0\leq j\leq l-1\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\alpha!\beta!}\frac{1}{2^{\left|\alpha\right|}}\frac{1}{(-2)^{\left|\beta\right|}}(\partial_{p}^{\alpha}D_{x}^{\beta}a_{\varepsilon,k})(\partial_{p}^{\beta}D_{x}^{\alpha}b_{\varepsilon,z,j})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle 0,

by definition of
b_{\varepsilon,z,l}
. These two equalities implies that

\sum_{k,j=0}^{N}\hbar^{k+j}\OpW(a_{\varepsilon,k})\OpW(b_{\varepsilon,z,j})=I+\hbar^{N+1}\OpW(\mathcal{R}_{\varepsilon}(a_{\varepsilon}(\hbar),B_{\varepsilon},z,N(\hbar);\hbar)).

(4.9)

This was the first part of equation (
4.8
). If we now
consider the second part of (
4.8
):

\sum_{j=0}^{N}\hbar^{N+1+j}R_{N}(\varepsilon,\hbar)\OpW(b_{\varepsilon,z,j}).

By Theorem
3.25
and
Lemma
4.6
there exist constants

M_{d}
and
C
such that

\hbar^{j}\lVert\OpW(b_{\varepsilon,z,j})\rVert_{\mathcal{L}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\leq C\left(\frac{\left|z-\zeta_{1}\right|}{\dist(z,[\zeta_{1},\infty))}\right)^{2j+M_{d}}

for all
j
in
\{0,\dots,N\}
. Hence by assumption we have

\sum_{j=0}^{N}\hbar^{N+1+j}\lVert R_{N}(\varepsilon,\hbar)\OpW(b_{\varepsilon,z,j})\rVert_{\mathcal{L}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\leq C\hbar^{\kappa(N)}\left(\frac{\left|z-\zeta_{1}\right|}{\dist(z,[\zeta_{1},\infty))}\right)^{q(N)}.

Now by combining this with (
4.8
) and
(
4.9
) we get

(A_{\varepsilon}(h)-z)\OpW B_{\varepsilon,z,N}=I+h^{N+1}\Delta_{z,N+1}(h)

with

\hbar^{N+1}\lVert\Delta_{z,N+1}(h)\rVert_{\mathcal{L}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\leq C\hbar^{\kappa(N)}\left(\frac{\left|z\right|}{\dist(z,[\zeta_{1},\infty))}\right)^{q(N)},

where
\kappa
is a positive strictly increasing function and
q(N)

is a positive integer depending on
N
. Which is the desired form and this ends the proof.
∎

4.3
Functional calculus for rough pseudo-differential operators

We are now almost ready to construct/prove a functional calculus for
operators satisfying Assumption
4.1
. First we
need to settle some terminology and recall a theorem.

Definition 4.7
.

For a smooth function
f:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
we define the almost
analytical extension
\tilde{f}:\mathbb{C}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
of
f
by

\tilde{f}(x+iy)=\left(\sum_{r=0}^{n}f^{(r)}(x)\frac{(iy)^{r}}{r!}\right)\sigma(x,y),

where
n\geq 1
and

\sigma(x,y)=\omega\left(\tfrac{y}{\lambda(x)}\right),

for some smooth function
\omega
, defined on
\mathbb{R}
such that

\omega(t)=1
for
\left|t\right|\leq 1
and
\omega(t)=0
for

\left|t\right|\geq 2
. Moreover we will use the notation

\displaystyle\bar{\partial}\tilde{f}(x+iy)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\partial\tilde{f}}{\partial x}+i\frac{\partial\tilde{f}}{\partial y}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{r=0}^{n}f^{(r)}(x)\frac{(iy)^{r}}{r!}\right)(\sigma_{x}(x,y)+i\sigma_{y}(x,y))+\frac{1}{2}f^{(n+1)}(x)\frac{(iy)^{n}}{n!}\sigma(x,y),

where
\sigma_{x}
and
\sigma_{y}
are the partial derivatives of

\sigma
with respect to
x
and
y
respectively.

Remark 4.8
.

The above choice is one way to define an almost analytic extension
and it is not unique. Once an
n
has been fixed the extension has
the property that

|\bar{\partial}\tilde{f}(x+iy)|=\mathcal{O}(\left|y\right|^{n})

as
y\rightarrow 0
. Hence when making calculation a choice has to be
made concerning how fast
|\bar{\partial}\tilde{f}|
vanishes when
approaching the real axis. If
f
is a
C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
function one
can find an almost analytic extension
\tilde{f}
in

C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{C})
such
f(x)=\tilde{f}(x)
for
x
in
\mathbb{R}
and

|\bar{\partial}\tilde{f}(x+iy)|\leq C_{N}\left|y\right|^{N},\quad\text{for all }N\geq 0.

without chancing the extension. This type of extension could be
based on a Fourier transform hence it may not work for a general
smooth function. For details see
[
6
, Chapter 8]
or
[
32
, Chapter 3]
.

The type of functions for which we can construct a functional calculus
is introduced in the next definition:

Definition 4.9
.

For
\rho
in
\mathbb{R}
we define the set
S^{\rho}
to be the set of
smooth functions
f:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
such that

|f^{(r)}(x)|\coloneqq\Big|\frac{d^{r}f}{dx^{r}}(x)\Big|\leq c_{r}\lambda(x)^{\rho-r}

for some
c_{r}<\infty
, all
x
in
\mathbb{R}
and all integers
r\geq 0
. Moreover we define

\mathcal{W}
by

\mathcal{W}\coloneqq\bigcup_{\rho<0}S^{\rho}.

We can now recall the form of the spectral theorem which we will use:

Theorem 4.10

(The Helffer-Sjöstrand
formula)
.

Let
H
be a self-adjoint operator acting on a Hilbert space

\mathscr{H}
and
f
a function from
\mathcal{W}
. Moreover let

\tilde{f}
be an almost analytic extension of
f
with
n

terms. Then the bounded operator
f(H)
is given by the equation

f(H)=-\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\bar{\partial}\tilde{f}(z)(z-H)^{-1}\,L(dz),

where
L(dz)=dxdy
is the Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{C}
. The formula
holds for all numbers
n\geq 1
.

A proof of the above theorem can be found in e.g.
[
6
]
or
[
5
]
. We are now ready to state and prove the functional calculus for a
certain class of rough pseudo-differential operators.

Theorem 4.11
.

Let
A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)
, for
\hbar
in
(0,\hbar_{0}]
, be a

\hbar
-
\varepsilon
-admissible operator of regularity

\tau\geq 1
and with symbol

a_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)=\sum_{j\geq 0}\hbar^{j}a_{\varepsilon,j}.

Suppose that
A_{\varepsilon}(h)
satisfies
Assumption
4.1
. Then for any function
f

from
\mathcal{W}
,
f(A_{\varepsilon}(h))
is a

\hbar
-
\varepsilon
-admissible operator of regularity
\tau
with
respect to a constant tempered weight
function.
f(A_{\varepsilon}(h))
has the symbol

a_{\varepsilon}^{f}(\hbar)=\sum_{j\geq 0}\hbar^{j}a_{\varepsilon,j}^{f},

where

\displaystyle a_{\varepsilon,0}^{f}

\displaystyle=f(a_{\varepsilon,0}),

(4.10)

\displaystyle a_{\varepsilon,j}^{f}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{2j-1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}d_{\varepsilon,j,k}f^{(k)}(a_{\varepsilon,0})\quad\quad\text{for $j\geq 1$},

the symbols
d_{\varepsilon,j,k}
are the polynomials from
Lemma
4.4
. Especially we have

a_{\varepsilon,1}^{f}=a_{\varepsilon,1}f^{(1)}(a_{\varepsilon,0}).

The proof is an application of Theorem
4.10
and
the fact that the resolvent is a
\hbar
-
\varepsilon
-admissible
operator as well.

Proof.

By Theorem
4.2
the operator

A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)
is essentially self-adjoint for sufficiently small
\hbar
. Hence
Theorem
4.10
gives us

f(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))=-\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\bar{\partial}\tilde{f}(z)(z-A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))^{-1}\,L(dz),

where
\tilde{f}
is an almost analytic extension of
f
. For the
almost analytic extension of
f
we will need a suffiently large
number of terms which we assume to have chosen from the
start. Theorem
4.3
gives that the resolvent
is a
\hbar
-
\varepsilon
-admissible operator and the explicit form
of it as well. Hence

\displaystyle f(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\bar{\partial}\tilde{f}(z)\sum_{j=0}^{M}\hbar^{j}\OpW(b_{\varepsilon,z,j})\,L(dz)-\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\bar{\partial}\tilde{f}(z)h^{N+1}(z-A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))^{-1}\Delta_{z,N+1}(h)\,L(dz),

where the symbols
b_{\varepsilon,z,j}
and the operator

\Delta_{z,N+1}(h)
are as defined in
Theorem
4.3
. If we start by considering the
error term we have by Theorem
4.3
the
estimate

\displaystyle\lVert(z-A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))^{-1}\Delta_{z,N+1}(h)\rVert_{\mathcal{L}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\leq{}

\displaystyle C\hbar^{\kappa(N)}\frac{1}{\left|\im(z)\right|}\left(\frac{\left|z\right|}{\dist(z,[\zeta_{1},\infty))}\right)^{q(N)}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C\hbar^{\kappa(N)}\frac{\left|z\right|^{q(N)}}{\left|\im(z)\right|^{q(N)+1}},

for
N
sufficiently large and where
q(N)
is some integer
dependent of the number
N
. We have

|\bar{\partial}\tilde{f}(z)|\leq c_{1}\sum_{r=0}^{n}|\tilde{f}^{(r)}(\re(z))|\lambda(\re(z))^{r-1}\bm{1}_{U}(z)+c_{2}|\tilde{f}^{(n+1)}(\re(z))||\im(z)|^{n}\bm{1}_{V}(z),

where

\displaystyle U

\displaystyle=\{z\in\mathbb{C}\,|\,\lambda(\re(z))<\left|\im(z)\right|<2\lambda(\re(z))\},

and

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle=\{z\in\mathbb{C}\,|\,0<\left|\im(z)\right|<2\lambda(\re(z))\}.

This estimate follows directly from the definition of

\tilde{f}
. By combining these estimates and the definition of the
class of functions
\mathcal{W}
we have

\Big\lVert\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\bar{\partial}\tilde{f}(z)h^{N+1}(z-A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))^{-1}\Delta_{z,N+1}(h)\,L(dz)\Big\rVert_{\mathcal{L}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\leq C\hbar^{\kappa(N)}.

What remains to prove the following equality

\sum_{j=0}^{M}\hbar^{j}\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\bar{\partial}\tilde{f}(z)\OpW(b_{\varepsilon,z,j})\,L(dz)=\sum_{j=0}^{M}\hbar^{j}\OpW(a_{\varepsilon,j}^{f}),

(4.11)

where the symbols
a_{\varepsilon,j}^{f}
are as defined in the
statement. We will only consider one of the terms as the rest is
treated analogously. Hence we need to establish the equality

\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\bar{\partial}\tilde{f}(z)\OpW(b_{\varepsilon,z,j})\,L(dz)=\OpW(a_{\varepsilon,j}^{f}).

As both operators are bounded we need only establish the equality
weekly for a dense subset of
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Hence let
\varphi
and

\psi
be two functions from
C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and a
j
be
given. We have

\langle\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\bar{\partial}\tilde{f}(z)\OpW(b_{\varepsilon,z,j})\,L(dz)\varphi,\psi\rangle=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\bar{\partial}\tilde{f}(z)\langle\OpW(b_{\varepsilon,z,j})\varphi,\psi\rangle\,L(dz),

(4.12)

where we have

\displaystyle\langle\OpW

\displaystyle(b_{\varepsilon,z,j})\varphi,\psi\rangle

(4.13)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3d}}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x-y,p\rangle}b_{\varepsilon,z,j}(\tfrac{x+y}{2},p)\varphi(y)\overline{\psi}(x)\,dydpdx

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\lim_{\sigma\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3d}}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x-y,p\rangle}g_{\sigma}(x,y,p)b_{\varepsilon,z,j}(\tfrac{x+y}{2},p)\varphi(y)\overline{\psi}(x)\,dydpdx,

where the function
g
is a positiv Schwartz function bounded by
1

and identical
1
in a neighbourhood of
0
. I the above we have set

g_{\sigma}(x,y,p)=g(\tfrac{x}{\sigma},\tfrac{y}{\sigma},\tfrac{p}{\sigma})
. The
next step in the proof is to apply dominated convergence to move the
limit outside the integral over
z
.

We let
\chi
be in
C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that
\chi(p)=1
for

\left|p\right|\leq 1
and
\chi(p)=0
for
\left|p\right|\geq 2
. With this
function we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3d}}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x-y,p\rangle}g_{\sigma}(x,y,p)b_{\varepsilon,z,j}(\tfrac{x+y}{2},p)\varphi(y)\overline{\psi}(x)\,dydpdx

(4.14)

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3d}}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x-y,p\rangle}g_{\sigma}(x,y,p)\chi(p)b_{\varepsilon,z,j}(\tfrac{x+y}{2},p)\varphi(y)\overline{\psi}(x)\,dydpdx

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3d}}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x-y,p\rangle}g_{\sigma}(x,y,p)(1-\chi(p))b_{\varepsilon,z,j}(\tfrac{x+y}{2},p)\varphi(y)\overline{\psi}(x)\,dydpdx\big].

By Lemma
4.6
we have

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3d}}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x-y,p\rangle}g_{\sigma}(x,y,p)\chi(p)b_{\varepsilon,z,j}(\tfrac{x+y}{2},p)\varphi(y)\overline{\psi}(x)\,dydpdx\Big|

(4.15)

\displaystyle\leq C_{j}\varepsilon^{-(\tau-j)_{-}}\left(\frac{\left|z-\zeta_{1}\right|}{\left|\im(z)\right|}\right)^{2j},

where the
\zeta_{1}
is the number from
Assumption
4.1
. The factor

\varepsilon^{-(\tau-j)_{-}}
is not an issue as the operator we
consider has
\hbar^{j}
in front. We have just omitted to write this
factor. This bound is clearly independent of
\sigma
. We now need
to bound the term with
1-\chi(p)
. Here we use that on the support
of
1-\chi(p)
we have
\left|p\right|>1
. Hence the operator

M=\frac{(-i\hbar)^{2d}}{\left|p\right|^{2d}}(\sum_{k=1}^{d}\partial_{y_{k}}^{2})^{d}=\frac{(-i\hbar)^{2d}}{\left|p\right|^{2d}}\sum_{\left|\alpha\right|=d}\partial_{y}^{2\alpha},

is well defined when acting on functions supported in

\supp(1-\chi)
. We then obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3d}}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x-y,p\rangle}g_{\sigma}(x,y,p)(1-\chi(p))b_{\varepsilon,z,j}(\tfrac{x+y}{2},p)\varphi(y)\overline{\psi}(x)\,dydpdx

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3d}}(Me^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x-y,p\rangle})g_{\sigma}(x,y,p)(1-\chi(p))b_{\varepsilon,z,j}(\tfrac{x+y}{2},p)\varphi(y)\overline{\psi}(x)\,dydpdx

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3d}}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x-y,p\rangle}(1-\chi(p))M^{t}(g_{\sigma}(x,y,p)b_{\varepsilon,z,j}(\tfrac{x+y}{2},p)\varphi(y))\overline{\psi}(x)\,dydpdx,

where we have used thet
Me^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x-y,p\rangle}=e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x-y,p\rangle}
. Just considering the action of
M^{t}
inside the integral we have by Leibniz’s formula

\displaystyle|M^{t}g_{\sigma}(x,y,p)b_{\varepsilon,z,j}(\tfrac{x+y}{2},p)\varphi(y)|

\displaystyle=\frac{\hbar^{2d}}{\left|p\right|^{2d}}\Big|\sum_{\left|\alpha\right|=d}\sum_{\beta\leq 2\alpha}\partial_{y}^{2\alpha-\beta}(g_{\sigma}(x,y,p)\varphi(y))\partial_{y}^{\beta}b_{\varepsilon,z,j}(\tfrac{x+y}{2},p)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{j}\frac{\hbar^{2d}}{\left|p\right|^{2d}}\bm{1}_{\supp(\varphi)}(y)\sum_{\left|\alpha\right|=d}\sum_{\beta\leq 2\alpha}\varepsilon^{-(\tau-j-\left|\beta\right|)_{-}}\left(\frac{\left|z-\zeta_{1}\right|}{\left|\im(z)\right|}\right)^{2j+\left|\beta\right|}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\hbar^{2d}}{\left|p\right|^{2d}}\varepsilon^{-(\tau-j-2d)_{-}}\bm{1}_{\supp(\varphi)}(y)\left(1+\frac{\left|z-\zeta_{1}\right|}{\left|\im(z)\right|}\right)^{2j+2d},

where we again have used
Lemma
4.6
. This imply the estimate

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3d}}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x-y,p\rangle}g_{\sigma}(x,y,p)(1-\chi(p))b_{\varepsilon,z,j}(\tfrac{x+y}{2},p)\varphi(y)\overline{\psi}(x)\,dydpdx\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{j}\varepsilon^{-(\tau-j)_{-}}\left(1+\frac{\left|z-\zeta_{1}\right|}{\left|\im(z)\right|}\right)^{2j+2d}.

If we combine this estimate with (
4.14
) and
(
4.15
) we have

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3d}}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x-y,p\rangle}g_{\sigma}(x,y,p)b_{\varepsilon,z,j}(\tfrac{x+y}{2},p)\varphi(y)\overline{\psi}(x)\,dydpdx\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{-(\tau-j)_{-}}\left(1+\frac{\left|z-\zeta_{1}\right|}{\left|\im(z)\right|}\right)^{2j+2d}.

As above we have

\Big|\int_{\mathbb{C}}\bar{\partial}\tilde{f}(z)\left(1+\frac{\left|z-\zeta_{1}\right|}{\left|\im(z)\right|}\right)^{2j+2d}\,L(dz)\Big|<\infty.

Hence we can apply dominated convergence and by an analogous
argument we can also apply Fubini’s Theorem. This gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\bar{\partial}\tilde{f}(z)\langle\OpW(b_{\varepsilon,z,j})\varphi,\psi\rangle\,L(dz)

(4.16)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\lim_{\sigma\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3d}}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x-y,p\rangle}g_{\sigma}(x,y,p)\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\bar{\partial}\tilde{f}(z)b_{\varepsilon,z,j}(\tfrac{x+y}{2},p)\,L(dz)\varphi(y)\overline{\psi}(x)\,dydpdx

If we only consider the integral over
z
then we have by a Cauchy
formula and Lemma
4.4
that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\bar{\partial}\tilde{f}(z)b_{\varepsilon,z,j}(\tfrac{x+y}{2},p)\,L(dz)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\bar{\partial}\tilde{f}(z)\sum_{k=1}^{2j-1}d_{\varepsilon,j,k}(\tfrac{x+y}{2},p)b_{\varepsilon,z,0}^{k+1}(\tfrac{x+y}{2},p)\,L(dz)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{2j-1}d_{\varepsilon,j,k}(\tfrac{x+y}{2},p)\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\bar{\partial}\tilde{f}(z)\Big(\frac{1}{a_{\varepsilon,0}(\tfrac{x+y}{2},p)-z}\Big)^{k+1}\,L(dz)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{2j-1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}d_{\varepsilon,j,k}(\tfrac{x+y}{2},p)f^{(k)}(a_{\varepsilon,0}(\tfrac{x+y}{2},p))=a_{\varepsilon,j}^{f}(\tfrac{x+y}{2},p),

which is the desired form of
a_{\varepsilon,j}^{f}
given in
(
4.10
). Now combing this with (
4.12
),
(
4.13
) and (
4.16
) we arrive at

\langle\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\bar{\partial}\tilde{f}(z)\OpW(b_{\varepsilon,z,j})\,L(dz)\varphi,\psi\rangle=\langle\OpW(a_{\varepsilon,j}^{f})\varphi,\psi\rangle.

The remaning
j
’ can be treated analogously and hence we obtain the
equality (
4.11
). This ends the proof.
∎

4.4
Applications of functional calculus

With the functional calculus we can now prove some useful theorems and lemmas. One of them is an asymptotic expansion of
certain traces.

Theorem 4.12
.

Let
A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)
, for
\hbar
in
(0,\hbar_{0}]
, be a

\hbar
-
\varepsilon
-admissible operator of regularity

\tau\geq 1
and symbol

a_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)=\sum_{j\geq 0}\hbar^{j}a_{\varepsilon,j}.

Suppose that
A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)
satisfies
Assumption
4.1
. Let
E_{1}<E_{2}
be two real
numbers and suppose there exists an
\eta>0
such

a_{\varepsilon,0}^{-1}([E_{1}-\eta,E_{2}+\eta])
is compact. Then we have

\spec(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))\cap[E_{1}.E_{2}]\subseteq\spec_{pp}(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)),

(4.17)

for
\hbar
sufficiently small, where
\spec_{pp}(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))
is the pure point spectrum of
A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)
.

Proof.

Let
f
and
g
be in

C_{0}^{\infty}((E_{1}-\eta,E_{2}+\eta))
such
g(t)=1
for

t\in[E_{1},E_{2}]
and
f(t)=1
for
t
in
\supp(g)
. By Theorem
4.11
we have

f(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))=A_{\varepsilon,f,N}(\hbar)+\hbar^{N+1}R_{N+1,f}(\varepsilon,\hbar),

(4.18)

where the terms
A_{\varepsilon,f,N}(\hbar)
consists of the first
N
terms in the
expansion in
\hbar
of
f(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))
. We get by (
4.18
) and the definition of
g
and
f
that

g(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))(I-\hbar^{N+1}R_{N+1,f}(\varepsilon,\hbar))=g(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))A_{\varepsilon,f,N}(\hbar).

Hence for
\hbar
sufficiently small we have

g(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))=g(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))A_{\varepsilon,f,N}(\hbar)(I-\hbar^{N+1}R_{N+1,f}(\varepsilon,\hbar))^{-1},

thereby we have the inequality

\lVert g(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))\rVert_{\Tr}\leq c\lVert g\rVert_{\infty}\lVert A_{\varepsilon,f,N}(\hbar)\rVert_{\Tr}\leq C\hbar^{-d},

(4.19)

where we have used Theorem
3.26
. Since
\bm{1}_{[E_{1},E_{2}]}(t)\leq g(t)
we have that
\bm{1}_{[E_{1},E_{2}]}(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))
is a trace class operator by (
4.19
). This implies the inclusion

\spec(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))\cap[E_{1}.E_{2}]\subseteq\spec_{pp}(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)),

for
\hbar
sufficiently small. This ends the proof.
∎

Theorem 4.13
.

Let
A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)
, for
\hbar
in
(0,\hbar_{0}]
, be a

\hbar
-
\varepsilon
-admissible operator of regularity

\tau\geq 1
and symbol

a_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)=\sum_{j\geq 0}\hbar^{j}a_{\varepsilon,j}.

Suppose that
A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)
satisfies
Assumption
4.1
. Let
E_{1}<E_{2}
be two real
numbers and suppose there exists an
\eta>0
such

a_{\varepsilon,0}^{-1}([E_{1}-\eta,E_{2}+\eta])
is compact. Then
for every
f
in
C_{0}^{\infty}((E_{1},E_{2}))
and any
N_{0}
in

\mathbb{N}
there exists an
N
in
\mathbb{N}
such that

|\Tr[f(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))]-\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\sum_{j=0}^{N}\hbar^{j}T_{j}(f,A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))|\leq C\hbar^{N_{0}+1-d}.

for all sufficiently small
\hbar
, where the
T_{j}
’s is given by

T_{j}(f,A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}\sum_{k=1}^{2j-1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}d_{\varepsilon,j,k}f^{(k)}(a_{\varepsilon,0})\,dxdp,

where
d_{\varepsilon,j,k}
are the polynomials from
Lemma
4.4
. In particular we have

\displaystyle T_{0}(f,A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))={}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}f(a_{\varepsilon,0})\,dxdp

and

\displaystyle T_{1}(f,A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}a_{\varepsilon,1}f^{(1)}(a_{\varepsilon,0})\,dxdp.

The proof is an application of Theorem
4.11
which gives
the form of the operator
f(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))
combined with the
trace formula from Theorem
3.27
and we use some of the same ideas as in the proof of Theorem
4.12
.

Proof.

Let
f
in
C_{0}^{\infty}((E_{1},E_{2}))
be given and fix a
g
in

C_{0}^{\infty}((E_{1}-\eta,E_{2}+\eta))
such that
g(t)=1
for

t\in[E_{1},E_{2}]
. By Theorem
4.11
we have

\displaystyle f(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))=A_{\varepsilon,f,N}(\hbar)+\hbar^{N+1}R_{N+1,f}(\varepsilon,\hbar),

and

\displaystyle g(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))=A_{\varepsilon,g,N}(\hbar)+\hbar^{N+1}R_{N+1,g}(\varepsilon,\hbar),

where the terms
A_{\varepsilon,f,N}(\hbar)
and

A_{\varepsilon,g,N}(\hbar)
consist of the first
N
terms in the
expansion in
\hbar
of
f(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))
and

g(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))
respectively. Since

f(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))g(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))=f(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))
we have

\displaystyle f(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))

\displaystyle=(A_{\varepsilon,f,N}(\hbar)+\hbar^{N+1}R_{N+1,f}(\varepsilon,\hbar))(A_{\varepsilon,g,N}(\hbar)+\hbar^{N+1}R_{N+1,g}(\varepsilon,\hbar))

\displaystyle=A_{\varepsilon,f,N}(\hbar)A_{\varepsilon,g,N}(\hbar)+\hbar^{N+1}[A_{\varepsilon,f,N}(\hbar)R_{N+1,g}(\varepsilon,\hbar)+R_{N+1,f}(\varepsilon,\hbar)A_{\varepsilon,g,N}(\hbar)].

By Theorem
3.26
we have that

\lVert f(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))-A_{\varepsilon,f,N}(\hbar)A_{\varepsilon,g,N}(\hbar)\rVert_{\Tr}\leq C\hbar^{\kappa(N)-d}

(4.20)

as
\hbar\rightarrow 0
. Hence taking
N
sufficiently large we can
consider the composition of the operators

A_{\varepsilon,f,N}(\hbar)A_{\varepsilon,g,N}(\hbar)
instead of

f(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))
. By the choice of
g
and
Theorem
3.24
(composition of operators) we
have

\displaystyle A_{\varepsilon,f,N}(\hbar)A_{\varepsilon,g,N}(\hbar)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{N}\hbar^{j}\OpW(a_{\varepsilon,j}^{f})+\hbar^{N+1}R_{N,f,g}(\varepsilon,\hbar,a_{\varepsilon})

(4.21)

\displaystyle=A_{\varepsilon,f,N}(\hbar)+\hbar^{N+1}R_{N,f,g}(\varepsilon,\hbar,a_{\varepsilon}).

Hence we have Theorem
3.26
that

\lVert A_{\varepsilon,f,N}(\hbar)-A_{\varepsilon,f,N}(\hbar)A_{\varepsilon,g,N}(\hbar)\rVert_{\Tr}\leq C\hbar^{\kappa(N)-d},

(4.22)

where we have used that the error term in (
4.21
) is a
\hbar
-pseudo-differential operator, which follows from Theorem
3.24
. Theorem
3.27
now gives

\displaystyle\Tr[A_{\varepsilon,f,N}(\hbar)]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{N}\hbar^{j}\Tr[\OpW(a_{\varepsilon,j}^{f})]

(4.23)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\sum_{j=0}^{N}\hbar^{j}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}\sum_{k=1}^{2j-1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}d_{\varepsilon,j,k}f^{(k)}(a_{\varepsilon,0})\,dxdp.

By combining (
4.20
) and (
4.22
) implies that

\left|\Tr[f(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))]-\Tr[A_{\varepsilon,f,N}(\hbar)]\right|\leq C\hbar^{\kappa(N)-2d-1}.

(4.24)

Hence by choosing
N
sufficiently large and combining (
4.23
) and (
4.24
) we get the desired estimate.
∎

The next Lemmas will be use-full in the proof of the Weyl
law. Both of these Lemmas are proven by applying the functional
calculus and the results on compositions of operators.

Lemma 4.14
.

Let
A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)
, for
\hbar
in
(0,\hbar_{0}]
, be a

\hbar
-
\varepsilon
-admissible operator of regularity

\tau\geq 1
and symbol

a_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)=\sum_{j\geq 0}\hbar^{j}a_{\varepsilon,j}.

Suppose that
A_{\varepsilon}(h)
satisfies
Assumption
4.1
. Let
E_{1}<E_{2}
be two real
numbers and suppose there exists an
\eta>0
such

a_{\varepsilon,0}^{-1}([E_{1}-\eta,E_{2}+\eta])
is compact. Let
f

be in
C_{0}^{\infty}((E_{1},E_{2}))
and suppose
\theta
is in

C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}_{p})
such

\supp(\theta)\subset a_{\varepsilon,0}^{-1}([E_{1}-\eta,E_{2}+\eta])
and
\theta(x,p)=1
for all
(x,p)
in

\supp(f(a_{0,\varepsilon}))
. Then we have the bound

\lVert(1-\OpW(\theta))f(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))\rVert_{\Tr}\leq C_{N}\hbar^{N}

for every
N
in
\mathbb{N}

Proof.

We choose
g
in
C_{0}^{\infty}((E_{1},E_{2}))
such
g(t)f(t)=f(t)
. We
now have

\lVert(1-\OpW(\theta))f(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))\rVert_{\Tr}\leq\lVert(1-\OpW(\theta))f(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))\rVert_{\mathcal{L}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\lVert g(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))\rVert_{\Tr}

By arguing as in the proof of Theorem
4.13
and applying
Theorem
3.26
we get

\lVert g(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))\rVert_{\Tr}\leq C_{d}\hbar^{-d}.

Form Theorem
4.11

f(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))
is

\hbar
-
\varepsilon
-admissible operator with symbols

a_{\varepsilon}^{f}(\hbar)=\sum_{j\geq 0}\hbar^{j}a_{\varepsilon,j}^{f},

where

a_{\varepsilon,j}^{f}=\sum_{k=1}^{2j-1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}d_{\varepsilon,j,k}f^{(k)}(a_{\varepsilon,0}),

the symbols
d_{\varepsilon,j,k}
are the polynomials from
Lemma
4.4
. The support of these
functions is disjoint from the support of symbol for the operator

(1-\OpW(\theta))
. Hence by Theorem
3.24
we
get the desired estimate.
∎

Lemma 4.15
.

Let
A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)
, for
\hbar
in
(0,\hbar_{0}]
, be a

\hbar
-
\varepsilon
-admissible operator of regularity

\tau\geq 1
and symbol

a_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)=\sum_{j\geq 0}\hbar^{j}a_{\varepsilon,j}.

Suppose that
A_{\varepsilon}(h)
satisfies
Assumption
4.1
. Let
E_{1}<E_{2}
be two real
numbers and suppose there exists an
\eta>0
such

a_{\varepsilon,0}^{-1}([E_{1}-\eta,E_{2}+\eta])
is compact. Suppose

\theta
is in
C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}_{p})
such

\supp(\theta)\subset a_{\varepsilon,0}^{-1}((E_{1},E_{2}))
.

Then for every
f
in
C_{0}^{\infty}([E_{1}-\eta,E_{2}+\eta])
such
f(t)=1
for all

t
in
[E_{1}-\frac{\eta}{2},E_{2}+\frac{\eta}{2}]
the bound

\lVert\OpW(\theta)(1-f(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)))\rVert_{\mathcal{L}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\leq C_{N}\hbar^{N},

is true for every
N
in
\mathbb{N}

Proof.

Theorem
4.11
gives us that
f(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))

is
\hbar
-
\varepsilon
-admissible operator with symbols

a_{\varepsilon}^{f}(\hbar)=\sum_{j\geq 0}\hbar^{j}a_{\varepsilon,j}^{f},

where

a_{\varepsilon,j}^{f}=\sum_{k=1}^{2j-1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}d_{\varepsilon,j,k}f^{(k)}(a_{\varepsilon,0}),

the symbols
d_{\varepsilon,j,k}
are the polynomials from
Lemma
4.4
. Hence we have that the
principal symbol of
(1-f(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)))
is

1-f(a_{\varepsilon,0})
. By assumption we then have that the
support of
\theta
and the support of every symbol in

f(A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar))
are disjoint. Hence
Theorem
3.24
implies the desired estimate.
∎

5
Microlocal approximation and properties of
propagators

In this section we will study the solution to the operator valued
Cauchy problem:

\begin{cases}\hbar\partial_{t}U(t,\hbar)-iU(t,\hbar)A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)=0&t\neq 0\\
U(0,\hbar)=\theta(x,\hbar D)&t=0,\end{cases}

where
A_{\varepsilon}
is self-adjoint and the symbol
\theta
is in

C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}_{p})
. In particular we will only consider
the case where
A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)
is a

\hbar
-
\varepsilon
-admissible operator of regularity
\tau\geq 1

which satisfies Assumption
4.1
. Hence for
sufficiently small
\hbar
the operator
A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)
is essentially
self-adjoint by Theorem
4.2
. It is well-known
that the solution to the operator valued Cauchy problem is the micro
localised propagator

\theta(x,\hbar D)e^{i\hbar^{-1}tA_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)}
.

We are interested in the propagators as they turn up in a smoothing of
functions applied to the operators we consider. In this smoothing
procedure we need to understand the behaviour of the propagator for
t
in a
small interval around zero. Usually this is done by constructing a specific
Fourier integral operator (FIO) as an approximation to propagator. In the “usual” construction the phase function is the solution to the Hamilton-Jacobi equations associated to the principal symbol. For
our set up this FIO approximation is not desirable as we can not
control the number of derivatives in the space variables and hence we
can not be certain about how the operator behave. Instead we will use
a different microlocal approximation for times in

[-\hbar^{1-\frac{\delta}{2}},\hbar^{1-\frac{\delta}{2}}]
.

The construction of the approximation is recursive and inspired by the
construction in the works of Zielinski. If the construction is
compered to the approximation in the works of Ivrii, one can note that
Ivrii’s construction is successive.

Our objective is to construct the approximation
U_{N}(t,\hbar)
such
that

\lVert\hbar\partial_{t}U_{N}(t,\hbar)-iU_{N}(t,\hbar)A_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))},

is small and the trace of the operator has the
?
right
?
asymptotic
behaviour. The kernel of the
approximation will have the following form

(x,y)\rightarrow\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x-y,p\rangle}e^{it\hbar^{-1}a_{\varepsilon}(x,p)}\sum_{j=0}^{N}(it\hbar^{-1})^{j}u_{j}(x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)\,dp,

where
N
is chosen such that the error is of a desired order, and
the
u_{j}
’s are compactly supported rough functions in
x
and
p
. A priori these operators looks singular in the sense that for each term in the sum we have an increasing power of the factor
\hbar^{-1}
. What we will see in the following Theorem is that the since each power of
\hbar^{-1}
comes with a power of
t
, then for
t
in
[-\hbar^{1-\frac{\delta}{2}},\hbar^{1-\frac{\delta}{2}}]
it has the desired properties.

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)
be a
\hbar
-
\varepsilon
-admissible
operator of regularity
\tau\geq 1
with tempered weight
m
which is
self-adjoint for all
\hbar
in
(0,\hbar_{0}]
, for
\hbar_{0}>0
and with

\varepsilon\geq\hbar^{1-\delta}
for a
\delta\in(0,1)
. Let

\theta(x,p)
be a function in

C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}_{p})
. Then for all
N_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
there
exist an operator
U_{N}(t,\varepsilon,\hbar)
with integral kernel

\displaystyle K_{U_{N}}(x,y,t

\displaystyle,\varepsilon,\hbar)=\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x-y,p\rangle}e^{it\hbar^{-1}a_{\varepsilon,0}(x,p)}\sum_{j=0}^{N}(it\hbar^{-1})^{j}u_{j}(x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)\,dp,

such that
K_{U_{N}}(x,y,0,\varepsilon,\hbar)
is the kernel of the
operator
\Opdef_{\hbar,0}(\theta)=\theta(x,\hbar D)
. The terms in the sum
satisfies
u_{0}(x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)=\theta(x,p)
,

u_{j}(x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{x}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}_{p}^{d})

and they satisfies the bounds

\left|\partial_{x}^{\beta}\partial_{p}^{\alpha}u_{j}(x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)\right|\leq\begin{cases}C_{\alpha\beta}&j=0\\
C_{\alpha\beta}\hbar\varepsilon^{-\left|\beta\right|}&j=1\\
C_{\alpha\beta}\hbar^{1+\delta(j-2)}\varepsilon^{-\left|\beta\right|}&j\geq 2\end{cases}

for all
\alpha
and
\beta
in
\mathbb{N}^{d}
in the case
\tau=1
. For

\tau\geq 2
the
u_{j}
’s satisfies the bounds

\left|\partial_{x}^{\beta}\partial_{p}^{\alpha}u_{j}(x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)\right|\leq\begin{cases}C_{\alpha\beta}&j=0\\
C_{\alpha\beta}\hbar\varepsilon^{-\left|\beta\right|}&j=1,2\\
C_{\alpha\beta}\hbar^{2+\delta(j-3)}\varepsilon^{-\left|\beta\right|}&j\geq 3\end{cases}

for all
\alpha
and
\beta
in
\mathbb{N}^{d}
. Moreover
U_{N}
satisfies the
following bound:

\lVert\hbar\partial_{t}U_{N}(t,\hbar)-iU_{N}(t,\hbar)A_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\leq C\hbar^{N_{0}}

for
\left|t\right|\leq\hbar^{1-\frac{\delta}{2}}
.

Remark 5.2
.

If the operator satisfies Assumption
4.1
,
then by Theorem
4.2
the operator will be essentially
self-adjoint for all sufficiently small
\hbar
. Hence
Assumption
4.1
would be sufficient but not
necessary for the above theorem to be true.
The number
N
is explicit dependent on
N_{0}
,
d
and
\delta
which follows directly from the proof.

Proof.

We start by fixing
N
such that

\min\big(1+\delta\big(\tfrac{N}{2}-1\big)-d,2+\delta\big(\tfrac{N}{2}-3\big)-d\big)\geq N_{0}.

By assumption we have for sufficiently large
M
in
\mathbb{N}
the
following form of
A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)

A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)=\sum_{j=0}^{M}\hbar^{j}\OpW(a_{\varepsilon,j})+\hbar^{M+1}R_{M}(\varepsilon,\hbar).

(5.1)

We can choose and fix
M
such the following estimate is true

\hbar^{M+1}\lVert R_{M}(\varepsilon,\hbar)\rVert_{\mathcal{L}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\leq C_{M}\hbar^{N_{0}}.

With this
M
we consider the sum in the expression of

A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)
. By
Corollary
3.20
there exists a sequence

\{\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon,j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
of symbols where

\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon,j}
is of regularity
\tau-j
and a

\tilde{M}
such

\sum_{j=0}^{M}\hbar^{j}\OpW(a_{\varepsilon,j})=\sum_{j=0}^{\tilde{M}}\hbar^{j}\Opdef_{\hbar,1}(\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon,j})+\hbar^{\tilde{M}+1}\tilde{R}_{\tilde{M}}(\varepsilon,\hbar),

(5.2)

where
a_{\varepsilon,0}=\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon,0}
and

\hbar^{\tilde{M}+1}\lVert\tilde{R}_{\tilde{M}}(\varepsilon,\hbar)\rVert_{\mathcal{L}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\leq C_{\tilde{M}}\hbar^{N_{0}}.

We will for the reminder of the proof use the notation

\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,p)=\sum_{j=0}^{\tilde{M}}\hbar^{j}\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon,j}(x,p).

(5.3)

The function
\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,p)
is a rough function of
regularity
\tau
. These choices and definitions will become
important again at the end of the proof.

For our fixed
N
we define the operator

\hbar\partial_{t}-i\mathcal{P}_{N}:C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}_{p}^{d})\rightarrow C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}_{p}^{d})
, where

\mathcal{P}_{N}b(t,x,p)=\sum_{\left|\alpha\right|\leq N}\frac{(-i\hbar)^{\left|\alpha\right|}}{\alpha!}\partial_{p}^{\alpha}\{b(t,x,p)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon}(x,p)\}

for a
b\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}_{p})
. First step is
to observe how the operator
\hbar\partial_{t}-i\mathcal{P}_{N}

acts on
e^{it\hbar^{-1}a_{\varepsilon,0}(x,p)}\psi(x,p)
for

\psi\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}_{p})
. We will in the following
calculation omit the dependence of the variables
x
and
p
. By
Leibniz’s formula and the chain rule we get

\displaystyle(\hbar\partial_{t}-i\mathcal{P}_{N})e^{it\hbar^{-1}a_{\varepsilon,0}}\psi=\hbar\partial_{t}e^{it\hbar^{-1}a_{\varepsilon,0}}\psi-i\sum_{\left|\alpha\right|\leq N}\frac{(-i\hbar)^{\left|\alpha\right|}}{\alpha!}\partial_{p}^{\alpha}\{e^{it\hbar^{-1}a_{\varepsilon,0}}\psi\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon}\}

(5.4)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle e^{it\hbar^{-1}a_{\varepsilon,0}}\Big[i\psi(a_{\varepsilon,0}-\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon})-i\sum_{\left|\alpha\right|=1}^{N}\frac{(-i\hbar)^{\left|\alpha\right|}}{\alpha!}\partial_{p}^{\alpha}\{\psi\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon}\}

\displaystyle-i\sum_{k=1}^{N}(it\hbar^{-1})^{k}\sum_{\left|\alpha\right|=k}^{N}\frac{(-i\hbar)^{\left|\alpha\right|}}{\alpha!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1}+\cdots+\beta_{k}\leq\alpha\\
\left|\beta_{j}\right|>0\end{subarray}}c_{\alpha,\beta_{1}\cdots\beta_{k}}\prod_{j=1}^{k}\partial_{p}^{\beta_{j}}a_{\varepsilon,0}\partial_{p}^{\alpha-(\beta_{1}+\cdots+\beta_{k})}\{\psi\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon}\}\Big]

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle ie^{it\hbar^{-1}a_{\varepsilon,0}}\sum_{k=0}^{N}(it\hbar^{-1})^{k}q_{k}(\psi,x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon).

From this we note after acting with

\hbar\partial_{t}-i\mathcal{P}_{N}
on

e^{it\hbar^{-1}a_{\varepsilon,0}(x,p)}\psi(x,p)
we get

ie^{it\hbar^{-1}a_{\varepsilon,0}}
times a polynomial in

it\hbar^{-1}
with coefficients in
C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}_{p})

depending on
\psi
,
\hbar
and
\varepsilon
. If we consider the
coefficients in the polynomial we have

\displaystyle|q_{0}(\psi,x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)|

\displaystyle=|\psi(a_{\varepsilon,0}-\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon})+\sum_{\left|\alpha\right|=1}^{N}\frac{(-i\hbar)^{\left|\alpha\right|}}{\alpha!}\partial_{p}^{\alpha}\{\psi\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon}\}|

(5.5)

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}\hbar+\sum_{\left|\alpha\right|=1}^{N}\frac{\hbar^{\left|\alpha\right|}}{\alpha!}\left|\partial_{p}^{\alpha}\{\psi\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon}\}\right|\leq c_{1}\hbar+\sum_{\left|\alpha\right|=1}^{\tau}\frac{\hbar^{\left|\alpha\right|}}{\alpha!}c_{\alpha}+\sum_{\left|\alpha\right|=\tau+1}^{N}\frac{\hbar^{\left|\alpha\right|}}{\alpha!}c_{\alpha}\varepsilon^{\tau-\left|\alpha\right|}

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}\hbar+\sum_{\left|\alpha\right|=1}^{\tau}\frac{\hbar^{\left|\alpha\right|}}{\alpha!}c_{\alpha}+\sum_{\left|\alpha\right|=\tau+1}^{N}\frac{\hbar^{\left|\alpha\right|}}{\alpha!}c_{\alpha}\hbar^{(1-\delta)(\tau-\left|\alpha\right|)}\leq C\hbar,

where
C
depends on the
p
-derivatives of
\psi
and

\partial_{x}^{\alpha}a_{\varepsilon}
on the support of
\psi
for

\left|\alpha\right|\leq N
. For
1\leq k\leq\tau
we have

\displaystyle|q_{k}(\psi,x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)|={}

\displaystyle|\sum_{\left|\alpha\right|=k}^{N}\frac{(-i\hbar)^{\left|\alpha\right|}}{\alpha!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1}+\cdots+\beta_{k}\leq\alpha\\
\left|\beta_{j}\right|>0\end{subarray}}c_{\alpha,\beta_{1}\cdots\beta_{k}}\prod_{j=1}^{k}\partial_{p}^{\beta_{j}}a_{\varepsilon,0}\partial_{p}^{\alpha-(\beta_{1}+\cdots+\beta_{k})}\{\psi\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon}\}|

(5.6)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\sum_{\left|\alpha\right|=k}^{\tau}c_{\alpha}\frac{\hbar^{\left|\alpha\right|}}{\alpha!}+\sum_{\left|\alpha\right|=\tau+1}^{N}c_{\alpha}\frac{\hbar^{\left|\alpha\right|}}{\alpha!}\varepsilon^{\tau-\left|\alpha\right|}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\sum_{\left|\alpha\right|=k}^{\tau}c_{\alpha}\frac{\hbar^{\left|\alpha\right|}}{\alpha!}+\sum_{\left|\alpha\right|=\tau+1}^{N}c_{\alpha}\frac{\hbar^{\left|\alpha\right|}}{\alpha!}\hbar^{(1-\delta)(\tau-\left|\alpha\right|)}\leq C\hbar^{k},

where
C
depends on the
p
-derivatives of
\psi
and

\partial_{x}^{\alpha}a_{\varepsilon}
on the support of
\psi
. For

\tau<k\leq N
we have

\displaystyle|q_{k}(\psi,x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)|={}

\displaystyle|\sum_{\left|\alpha\right|=k}^{N}\frac{(-i\hbar)^{\left|\alpha\right|}}{\alpha!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1}+\cdots+\beta_{k}\leq\alpha\\
\left|\beta_{j}\right|>0\end{subarray}}c_{\alpha,\beta_{1}\cdots\beta_{k}}\prod_{j=1}^{k}\partial_{p}^{\beta_{j}}a_{\varepsilon,0}\partial_{p}^{\alpha-(\beta_{1}+\cdots+\beta_{k})}\{\psi\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon}\}|

(5.7)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\sum_{\left|\alpha\right|=k}^{N}c_{\alpha}\frac{\hbar^{\left|\alpha\right|}}{\alpha!}\varepsilon^{\tau-\left|\alpha\right|}\leq\ \sum_{\left|\alpha\right|=k}^{N}c_{\alpha}\frac{\hbar^{\left|\alpha\right|}}{\alpha!}\hbar^{(1-\delta)(\tau-\left|\alpha\right|)}\leq C\hbar^{\tau+(k-\tau)\delta},

where
C
depends on the
p
-derivatives of
\psi
and

\partial_{x}^{\alpha}a_{\varepsilon}
on the support of
\psi
. It is
important that the coefficients only depends on derivatives in

p
for the function we apply the operator to. One should also note
that if
\psi
had
\hbar
to some power multiplied to it. Then it should
be multiplied to the new power obtained. In the reminder of the
proof we will continue to denote the coefficients obtained by acting
with
\hbar\partial_{t}-i\mathcal{P}_{N}
by
q_{j}
and the exact
form can be found in (
5.4
).

We are now ready to start constructing the kernel. We set

u_{0}(x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)=\theta(x,p)
which gives the first
term. In order to find
u_{1}
we act with the operator

\hbar\partial_{t}-i\mathcal{P}_{N}
on the function

e^{it\hbar^{-1}a_{\varepsilon,0}}u_{0}(x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)
,
where we in the reminder of the construction of the approximation
will omit writing the dependence of the variables
(x,p)
in the
exponential. By (
5.4
) we get

(\hbar\partial_{t}-i\mathcal{P}_{N})e^{it\hbar^{-1}a_{\varepsilon,0}}u_{0}(x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)=ie^{it\hbar^{-1}a_{\varepsilon,0}}\sum_{k=0}^{N}(it\hbar^{-1})^{k}q_{k}(u_{0},x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon).

This would not lead to the desired estimate. So we now take

u_{1}(x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)=-q_{0}(u_{0},x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon).

We can note by the previous estimates
(
5.5
) we have

\left|u_{1}(x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)\right|=\left|q_{0}(u_{0},x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)\right|\leq\hbar C.

(5.8)

Acting with the operator
\hbar\partial_{t}-i\mathcal{P}_{N}
on

e^{it\hbar^{-1}a_{\varepsilon,0}}(u_{0}(x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)+it\hbar^{-1}u_{1}(x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon))
we obtain according to
(
5.4
) that

\displaystyle(\hbar\partial_{t}-i\mathcal{P}_{N})(e^{it\hbar^{-1}a_{\varepsilon,0}}(u_{0}(x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)+it\hbar^{-1}u_{1}(x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)))

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle ie^{it\hbar^{-1}a_{\varepsilon,0}}\sum_{k=0}^{N}(it\hbar^{-1})^{k}q_{k}(u_{0},x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)+ie^{it\hbar^{-1}a_{\varepsilon,0}}u_{1}(x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle+it\hbar^{-1}ie^{it\hbar^{-1}a_{\varepsilon,0}}\sum_{k=0}^{N}(it\hbar^{-1})^{k}q_{k}(u_{1},x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle ie^{it\hbar^{-1}a_{\varepsilon,0}}(\sum_{k=1}^{N}(it\hbar^{-1})^{k}q_{k}(u_{0},x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)+\sum_{k=0}^{N}(it\hbar^{-1})^{k+1}q_{k}(u_{1},x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)).

Now taking

u_{2}(x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)=-\frac{1}{2}(q_{1}(u_{0},x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)+q_{0}(u_{1},x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon))

and acting with the operator
\hbar\partial_{t}-i\mathcal{P}_{N}
,
according to (
5.4
), we get that

\displaystyle(\hbar\partial_{t}

\displaystyle-i\mathcal{P}_{N})e^{it\hbar^{-1}a_{\varepsilon,0}}\sum_{j=0}^{2}(it\hbar^{-1})^{j}u_{j}(x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=ie^{it\hbar^{-1}a_{\varepsilon,0}}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{N}(it\hbar^{-1})^{k+j}q_{k}(u_{j},x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)+\sum_{j=1}^{2}j(it\hbar^{-1})^{j-1}u_{j}(x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)\Big]

\displaystyle=ie^{it\hbar^{-1}a_{\varepsilon,0}}\sum_{j=0}^{2}\sum_{k=2-j}^{N}(it\hbar^{-1})^{k+j}q_{k}(u_{j},x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon).

We note that the
?
lowest
?
power of
it\hbar^{-1}
is
2
. Hence it is these
terms which should be used to construct
u_{3}
. Moreover, we
note that by (
5.5
) and (
5.6
) we have

\left|u_{2}(x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)\right|=\tfrac{1}{2}\left|q_{1}(u_{0},x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)+q_{0}(u_{1},x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)\right|\leq\tfrac{1}{2}C(\hbar+\hbar^{2})\leq C\hbar,

(5.9)

and
u_{2}
is a smooth compactly supported function in the variables

x
and
p
. Generally for
2\leq j\leq N
we have

u_{j}(x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)=-\frac{1}{j}\sum_{k=0}^{j-1}q_{j-1-k}(u_{k},x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon).

We now need estimates for these terms. In the case
\tau=1
the next
step will be empty, but for
\tau\geq 2
it is needed. For

\tau\geq 2
we have

|u_{3}(x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)|\leq\frac{1}{3}\sum_{k=0}^{2}|q_{2-k}(u_{k},x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)|\leq C\hbar^{2},

where we have used (
5.5
), (
5.6
),
(
5.8
) and (
5.9
). For the rest of the
u_{j}
’s
we split in the two cases
\tau=1
or
\tau\geq 2
. First the cases

\tau=1
for
2\leq j\leq N
the estimate is

\left|u_{j}(x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)\right|\leq C\hbar^{1+\delta(j-2)}

Note that
u_{2}
satisfies the above equation hence if we assume it
okay for
j-1
between
2
and
N-1
we want to show the above
estimate for
j
. We note that for
j\geq 5

\displaystyle|{u_{j}(x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)}|

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{j}\sum_{k=0}^{j-1}\left|q_{j-1-k}(u_{k},x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)\right|

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C(\left|q_{j-1}(u_{0},x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)\right|+\left|q_{j-2}(u_{1},x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)\right|

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=2}^{j-3}\left|q_{j-1-k}(u_{k},x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)\right|+\left|q_{1}(u_{j-2},x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)\right|+\left|q_{0}(u_{j-1},x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)\right|)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C(\hbar^{1+\delta(j-2)}+\hbar^{2+\delta(j-3)}+\sum_{k=2}^{j-3}\hbar^{1+\delta(j-1-k-1)+1+\delta(k-2)}+\hbar^{2+\delta(j-4)}+\hbar^{2+\delta(j-3)})

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C(\hbar^{1+\delta(j-2)}+\hbar^{2+\delta(j-3)}+\hbar^{2+\delta(j-4)}+\hbar^{2+\delta(j-3)})\leq C\hbar^{1+\delta(j-2)},

where we
have used (
5.5
), (
5.7
) and the induction
assumption. The cases
j=3
and
j=4
are estimated analogously.

Now the case
\tau\geq 2
which we will treat as
\tau=2
, here the
estimate is

\left|u_{j}(x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)\right|\leq C\hbar^{2+\delta(j-3)}

for
3\leq j\leq N
. To prove this bound is the same as in the case
of
\tau=1
. In order to prove the bound with the derivatives as
stated in the theorem the above arguments are repeated with a number
of derivatives on the
u_{j}
’s otherwise it is analogous.

What remains is to prove this construction satisfies the bound

\lVert\hbar\partial_{t}U_{N}(t,\hbar)-iU_{N}(t,\hbar)A_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\mathcal{L}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\leq C\hbar^{N_{0}}

Here we only consider the case
\tau=1
as the cases
\tau\geq 2

will have better estimates. Hence from the above estimates we have
for
k
in
\{0,\dots,N\}
and
\left|t\right|\leq\hbar^{1-\frac{\delta}{2}}

\left|(it\hbar^{-1})^{k}u_{k}(x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)\right|\leq\begin{cases}C&k=0\\
C\hbar^{1-\frac{\delta}{2}}&k=1\\
C\hbar^{1+\delta(\frac{k}{2}-2)}&k\geq 2.\end{cases}

(5.10)

The first step is to apply the operator

\hbar\partial_{t}-i\mathcal{P}_{N}
on then
?
full
?
kernel and see
what error this produces. By construction we have

(\hbar\partial_{t}-i\mathcal{P}_{N})e^{it\hbar^{-1}a_{\varepsilon,0}}\sum_{k=0}^{N}(it\hbar^{-1})^{k}u_{k}(x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)=\sum_{j=0}^{N}\sum_{k=N-j}^{N}(it\hbar^{-1})^{k+j}q_{k}(u_{j},x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon).

If we start by considering
j
equal
0
and
1
we note that:

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sum_{k=N-j}^{N}(it\hbar^{-1})^{k+j}q_{k}(u_{j},x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)\Big|

(5.11)

\displaystyle\leq C(\hbar^{-\frac{\delta}{2}N}\hbar^{1+\delta(N-1)}+\hbar^{-\frac{\delta}{2}N}\hbar^{2+\delta(N-2)}+\hbar^{-\frac{\delta}{2}(N+1)}\hbar^{2+\delta(N-1)})

\displaystyle\leq C(\hbar^{1+\delta(\frac{N}{2}-1)}+\hbar^{2+\delta(\frac{N}{2}-2)}+\hbar^{2+\frac{\delta}{2}(N-3)})

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}\hbar^{1+\delta(\frac{N}{2}-1)}\leq\tilde{C}\hbar^{N_{0}+d},

where we have used (
5.10
) and our choice of
N
. For the rest of the terms we have that

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{j=2}^{N}\sum_{k=N-j}^{N}(it\hbar^{-1})^{k+j}q_{k}(u_{j},x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)\Big|

(5.12)

\displaystyle\leq C\Big[\sum_{j=2}^{N}\sum_{k=\max(N-j,1)}^{N}\hbar^{-\frac{\delta}{2}(k+j)}\hbar^{2+\delta(k-1)+\delta(j-2)}+\hbar^{-\frac{\delta}{2}N}\hbar^{2+\delta(N-2)}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\Big[\sum_{j=2}^{N}\sum_{k=\max(N-j,1)}^{N}\hbar^{2+\delta(\frac{j+k}{2}-3)}+\hbar^{2+\delta(\frac{N}{2}-2)}\Big]\leq\tilde{C}\hbar^{N_{0}+d},

where we have used (
5.10
), our choice of
N
and that in the double sum
k+j\geq N
. When (
5.11
) and (
5.12
) are combined we have that

|(\hbar\partial_{t}-i\mathcal{P}_{N})e^{it\hbar^{-1}a_{\varepsilon,0}}\sum_{k=0}^{N}(it\hbar^{-1})^{k}u_{k}(x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)|\leq C\hbar^{N_{0}+d}.

(5.13)

We now let
U_{N}(t,\hbar)
be the operator with the integral kernel:

K_{U_{N}}(x,y,t,\varepsilon,\hbar)=\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x-y,p\rangle}e^{it\hbar^{-1}a_{\varepsilon,0}(x,p)}\sum_{j=0}^{N}(it\hbar^{-1})^{j}u_{j}(x,p,\hbar,\varepsilon)\,dp,

which is well defined due to our previous estimates (
5.10
). In particular we have that it is a bounded operator
by the Schur test. We now need to find an expression for

\hbar\partial_{t}U_{N}(t,\hbar)-iU_{N}(t,\hbar)A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar).

In the start of the proof we wrote the operator

A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)
in two different ways (
5.1
) and
(
5.2
). If we combine these we have

\displaystyle A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{\tilde{M}}\hbar^{j}\Opdef_{\hbar,1}(\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon,j})+\hbar^{\tilde{M}+1}\tilde{R}_{\tilde{M}}(\varepsilon,\hbar)+\hbar^{M+1}R_{M}(\varepsilon,\hbar)

\displaystyle=\Opdef_{\hbar,1}(\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon})+\hbar^{\tilde{M}+1}\tilde{R}_{\tilde{M}}(\varepsilon,\hbar)+\hbar^{M+1}R_{M}(\varepsilon,\hbar),

where the two reminder terms satisfies

\lVert\hbar^{\tilde{M}+1}\tilde{R}_{\tilde{M}}(\varepsilon,\hbar)+\hbar^{M+1}R_{M}(\varepsilon,\hbar)\rVert_{\mathcal{L}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\leq C\hbar^{N_{0}}.

If we use this form of
A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)
we have

\displaystyle\hbar\partial_{t}U_{N}(t,\hbar)-iU_{N}(t,\hbar)A_{\varepsilon}(\hbar)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\hbar\partial_{t}U_{N}(t,\hbar)-iU_{N}(t,\hbar)\Opdef_{\hbar,1}(\tilde{a}_{\varepsilon})-i\hbar^{\tilde{M}+1}U_{N}(t,\hbar)\tilde{R}_{\tilde{M}}(\varepsilon,\hbar)-i\hbar^{M+1}U_{N}(t,\hbar)R_{M}(\varepsilon,\hbar).

When considering the operator norm of the two last terms we have

\lVert\hbar^{\tilde{M}+1}U_{N}(t,\hbar)\tilde{R}_{\tilde{M}}(\varepsilon,\hbar)+\hbar^{M+1}U_{N}(t,\hbar)R_{M}(\varepsilon,\hbar)\rVert_{\mathcal{L}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\leq C\hbar^{N_{0}}

(5.14)

as
U_{N}(t,\hbar)
is a bounded operator. What remains is the
expression
